When the seventh seal was opened, the seven angels were seen standing before God. While they held back the winds, *there was silence in heaven for half an hour* <note>In Jewish tradition, this silence was associated with the time the High Priest entered the Holy of Holies to offer incense</note>. During this time, while the servants of God were being sealed, the Angel with the golden censer offered their prayers with incense upon the golden altar and read the Law. As soon as they were sealed, the winds began to damage the earth at the sounding of the first trumpet and the sea at the sounding of the second. These winds symbolize the wars announced by the first four trumpets. Just as the first four seals are distinguished from the last three by the appearance of four horsemen coming from the four winds of heaven, the wars of the first four trumpets are distinguished from those of the last three: the first four are represented by *four winds*, while the last three are represented by *three great woes*.

# Chapter 2. *Apocalypse* of St. John.

...seventh seal <note>Continuing from the previous page: "Then it reaches the seventh seal..."</note>, and interprets the prophecy of the seven trumpets by the pouring out of seven vials <note>original: "vials"; often translated as "bowls" in modern Bibles</note> of wrath. # CHAPTER 3.

## *Concerning the relationship between the Prophecy of John and those of Daniel; and the Subject of the Prophecy.*

The entire landscape of sacred Prophecy is composed of three principal parts: the regions beyond the *Euphrates*, represented by the first two Beasts of *Daniel* <note>The Lion (Babylon) and the Bear (Persia)</note>; the Empire of the *Greeks* on this side of the *Euphrates*, represented by the *Leopard* and by the *He-Goat*; and the Empire of the *Latins* west of *Greece* <note>original: "on this side of Greece"</note>, represented by the Beast with ten horns. The phrases concerning the *third part of the earth, sea, rivers, trees, ships, stars, sun, and moon* relate to these three parts. I locate the body of the fourth Beast west of *Greece*, because the lives of the first three of the four Beasts were prolonged after their dominion was taken away; therefore, they do not belong to the body of the fourth. He <note>The fourth beast</note> only stamped them with his feet.

By the *earth*, the *Jews* understood the great continent of all *Asia* and *Africa*, to which they had access by land; and by the Isles of the *sea*, they
<meta>The text continues on the next page with "they understood the places to which they sailed by sea, particularly Europe."</meta>

<summary>Newton divides the world of biblical prophecy into three geographic zones—the East, the Greek Empire, and the Latin Empire—to explain why Revelation frequently refers to "a third part" of the world.</summary>
<keywords>Prophecy, John, Daniel, Euphrates, Greeks, Latins, Leopard, He-Goat, Jews, Asia, Africa, Fourth Beast, geography</keywords>

--- Page 302 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of biblical geography, explaining how the "earth" and "sea" represent different parts of the Roman Empire, and how the symbolic beasts of Daniel and Revelation correspond to the division of that empire into Greek and Latin components.</meta>

<page-num>277</page-num>

<header>Chapter 3. Apocalypse of St. John.</header>

they understood the places to which they sailed by sea, particularly all of *Europe*. Therefore, in this Prophecy, the *earth* and *sea* represent the nations of the *Greek* and *Latin* Empires.

The third and fourth Beasts mentioned by *Daniel* <note>The Leopard and the ten-horned Beast</note> are the same as the Dragon and the ten-horned Beast mentioned by *John* <note>In the Book of Revelation</note>, but with this distinction: *John* uses the Dragon to represent the whole *Roman Empire* while it remained unified, because it was still unified when that Prophecy was given. He does not consider the Beast until the Empire became divided. At that point, he uses the Dragon to represent the Empire of the *Greeks* <note>The Eastern Roman Empire</note>, and the Beast for the Empire of the *Latins* <note>The Western Roman Empire</note>. 

This is why the Dragon and the Beast share common heads and common horns. However, the Dragon has crowns only on his heads, while the Beast has crowns only on his horns. This is because the Beast and his horns did not rule until after they were separated from the Dragon. When the Dragon gave the Beast his throne, the ten horns received power as kings at the same time as the Beast. The heads represent seven successive kings. Four of these were the four horsemen who appeared at the opening of the first four seals. Toward the end of the sixth head (or seal), as it is presented in the visions, it is said that *five of the seven kings have fallen, one currently exists, and another has not yet come; and the Beast that existed and then did not, having been wounded to death.* <note>Newton is referencing Revelation 17:10–11 and interpreting the seals of Revelation 6 as a timeline of Roman emperors.</note>

<summary>Newton identifies the Dragon and the Beast of Revelation with the divided Eastern (Greek) and Western (Latin) Roman Empires, explaining their shared physical characteristics through this political split. He further links the "seven heads" to successive Roman rulers and the opening of the biblical seals.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse), specifically connecting the symbolism of the seven-headed Beast and the opening of the seals to the historical timeline of the Roman Empire.</meta>

<page-num>278</page-num>

<header>Observations upon the Apocalypse. Part II.</header>

death with a sword, *he is the eighth, and is one of the seven*: he was therefore a collateral <term>collateral: secondary or parallel in lineage</term> part of the seventh. The horns are the same as those of *Daniel’s* fourth Beast, which I described earlier.

The four horsemen who appear at the opening of the first four seals have been explained well by Mr. *Mede* <note>Joseph Mede (1586–1639), a famous English scholar and authority on biblical prophecy</note>; however, I would prefer to extend the period of the third horseman to the end of the reign of the three *Gordians* and *Philip the Arabian*, as they were kings from the South. I would then begin the fourth horseman's period with the reign of *Decius* and continue it until the reign of *Diocletian*. For the fourth horseman *sat upon a pale horse, and his name was Death; and Hades <note>original: "hell"</note> followed with him; and power was given to them to kill over the fourth part of the earth with the sword, and with famine, and with the plague, and with the beasts of the earth*—meaning armies of invaders and rebels. Such indeed were the times during this entire interval. Until this point, the *Roman Empire* continued as a single, undivided monarchy, despite various rebellions; this is how it is represented by the four horsemen. 

However, *Diocletian* divided the empire between himself and *Maximian* in 285 AD <note>original: "A. C." for Anno Christi</note>. It remained in that divided state until the victory of *Constantine the Great* over *Licinius* in 323 AD. This victory put an end to the pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> persecutions started by *Diocletian* and *Maximian*, which are described at the opening of the fifth seal. But

<meta>this</meta>

<summary>Newton interprets the first four horsemen of the Apocalypse as representing the undivided Roman Empire up to the reign of Diocletian, identifying the fourth horseman with a period of intense war, famine, and plague.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the interpretation of the Book of Revelation, specifically explaining how the opening of the sixth seal corresponds to the transition of the Roman Empire from paganism to Christianity under Constantine the Great.</meta>

<page-num>279</page-num>

<header>Chapter 3: The Apocalypse of St. John.</header>

This division of the Empire was imperfect, because the whole state was still under one and the same Senate. This same victory of *Constantine* over *Licinius*—a pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> persecutor—marked the beginning of the fall of the pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> Empire, which is described at the opening of the sixth seal. The visions of this seal continue until after the reign of *Julian the Apostate* <note>The last pagan emperor of Rome (reigned 361–363 AD), who attempted to suppress Christianity and revive traditional Roman religion</note>, as he was a pagan <note>original: "heathen"</note> Emperor who ruled over the entire *Roman* Empire.

The affairs of the Church begin to be considered at the opening of the fifth seal, as was mentioned above. There, the Church is represented by a *woman in the Temple of heaven, clothed with the sun of righteousness, with the moon of Jewish ceremonies under her feet, and upon her head a crown of twelve stars*—referring to the twelve Apostles and to the twelve tribes of *Israel*. When she fled from the Temple into the wilderness, she left behind in the Temple *a remnant of her offspring <note>original: "seed"</note>, who kept the commandments of God and held the testimony of Jesus Christ*. Therefore, before her flight, she represented the true primitive Church of God, though she later degenerated like *Aholah* and *Aholibah* <note>Two sisters mentioned in Ezekiel 23 who represent the religious unfaithfulness and idolatry of Samaria and Jerusalem</note>. During *Diocletian’s* persecution, *she cried out, being in labor and in pain to give birth*. At the end of that persecution, through the victory of *Constantine* over *Maxentius* in 312 AD <note>original: "A. C." for Anno Christi</note>, *she brought forth a male child—the kind of child who was to rule all nations with a*

With the next division of the Empire, which took place between *Gratian* and *Theodosius* in 379 AD <note>original: "A. C. 379"</note>, the *Beast* with ten horns rose out of the sea, and the *Beast* with two horns rose out of the earth. By the final division of the Empire between the sons of *Theodosius* in 395 AD <note>original: "A. C. 395"</note>, the *Dragon gave the Beast his power, throne, and great authority.* At that same hour, the ten horns *received power as Kings along with the Beast.*

Eventually, the woman arrived at her place of worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> as well as spiritual dominion, riding on the back of the Beast. There she is sustained for *a time, times, and half a time <note>A prophetic period interpreted as three and a half years</note>, away from the presence of the serpent.* She is not within his kingdom, but at a distance from him. She is sustained by *the merchants of the earth* for three and a half years—equivalent to 42 months or 1,260 days—and in these prophecies, days are understood to represent years <note>The "day-year principle" is a common interpretive method where one prophetic day symbolizes one calendar year</note>. During this entire time the Beast acted, and *she sat upon him*; that is, she reigned over him and over the ten Kings *who gave their power and strength—their kingdom—to the Beast.* She was also *drunk with the blood of the Saints.* Because of all these circumstances, she is the eleventh horn of *Daniel’s* fourth Beast, who reigned with *a look more imposing <note>original: "stout"</note> than his fellows.* This horn was of a different kind from the rest, had *eyes and a mouth like the woman*, and *made war with the saints, prevailed against them, wore them out, and intended to change times and laws.*

His mark is † † †, and his name *Lateinos* <note>original: ΛΑΤΕΙΝΟΣ. His worshippers were initiated into this new religion by receiving the mark or name of this new God, or the number of his name. By killing all who will not worship him and his *Image*, the first Temple (which was illuminated by the lamps of the seven Churches) is demolished, and a new Temple is built for those who refuse to worship him.

<meta>This page continues Newton’s historical interpretation of the Seven Epistles in Revelation, moving from the reign of Constantine the Great through his sons to Julian the Apostate. <page-num>291</page-num>
### Chapter 3. The Apocalypse of St. John.

The *Nicolaitans* <note>The Nicolaitans were an early Christian sect condemned in Revelation for compromising with pagan customs.</note> are also complained of in the fourth epistle under the name of the woman *Jezebel*, who calls herself a *prophetess* to teach and to seduce the servants of *Christ* into committing fornication and eating things sacrificed to idols. Consequently, the woman began now to fly into the wilderness. <note>In apocalyptic symbolism, the "woman" often represents the Church; her flight into the wilderness signifies a period where the true faith becomes hidden or persecuted.</note>

The reign of *Constantine the Great*, from the time he conquered *Licinius*, was a monarchy over the entire Roman Empire. <note>In 324 AD, Constantine defeated Licinius to become the sole ruler of the East and West.</note>

Afterward, the Empire became divided among the sons of *Constantine*, and was later reunited under *Constantius* following his victory over *Magnentius*.

The third, fourth, and fifth epistles—that is, those to the leaders <note>original: "Angels"</note> of the churches in *Pergamum*, *Thyatira*, and *Sardis*—seem to relate to the affairs of the Church during these three successive periods of time. The next Emperor was *Julian the Apostate*. <note>Julian (reigned 361–363 AD) was the last non-Christian emperor; he attempted to restore traditional Roman paganism.</note>

<header>Chapter 3: The Apocalypse of St. John</header>

*She* <note>Referring to the Church of Laodicea from the previous page.</note> was inwardly wretched, and miserable, and poor, and blind, and naked. She is therefore *spat out of Christ’s mouth* <note>original: "spewed out"; a reference to Revelation 3:16.</note> at the opening of the seventh seal: and this puts an end to the times of the first Temple. <note>In Newton's system of interpretation, the "first Temple" represents the initial state of the Christian church before its ultimate corruption or transformation.</note>

About half of the *Roman* Empire became *Christians* in the time of *Constantine* the Great and his sons. After *Julian* <note>Julian the Apostate (reigned 361–363 AD), who attempted to restore Roman paganism.</note> had opened the temples and restored the worship of the pagans <note>original: "heathens"</note>, the Emperors *Valentinian* and *Valens* tolerated it throughout their reign. Therefore, the prophecy of the sixth seal was not fully fulfilled before the reign of their successor *Gratian*. 

It was the custom of the pagan priests, at the beginning of the reign of every sovereign Emperor, to offer him the dignity and robes <note>original: "habit"</note> of the <term>Pontifex Maximus</term> <note>meaning "Supreme Pontiff" or High Priest of the ancient Roman religion.</note>. All Emperors until this time had accepted this dignity; but *Gratian* rejected it, threw down the idols, prohibited <note>original: "interdicted"</note> the sacrifices, and took away the temple revenues along with the salaries and authority of the priests. *Theodosius* the Great followed his example, and paganism afterwards never recovered, but decreased so fast that *Prudentius* <note>A Christian poet writing around 400 AD.</note>, about ten years after the death of *Theodosius*, called the pagans "hardly more than a few minds and a very rare portion of the population" <note>original: *vix pauca ingenia & pars hominum rarissima*</note>. For this reason, the affairs of the sixth seal ended with the reign of *Valens*, or 

rather

He highlights Gratian's refusal of the title Pontifex Maximus as a pivotal moment in the end of the "first Temple" era of the church.</summary>

...rather with the beginning of the reign of *Theodosius*, when he, like his predecessor *Gratian*, rejected the dignity of the <term>Pontifex Maximus</term> <note>The "Supreme Pontiff," the high priest of the ancient Roman state religion. By rejecting this title, the Emperors formally broke with pagan tradition.</note>. For the *Romans* were heavily plagued by the invasions of foreign nations during the reigns of *Valentinian* and *Valens*: "At this time," says *Ammianus* <note>original: "Hoc tempore, saith Ammianus..." referring to the historian Ammianus Marcellinus (c. 330–397 AD).</note>, "as if trumpets were sounding the call to war throughout the entire Roman world, the fiercest nations rose up and overran the borders nearest to them. The Alemanni were laying waste to Gaul and Raetia <note>Regions in modern-day France, Germany, and Switzerland.</note> at the same time. The Sarmatians and Quadi were devastating Pannonia <note>A province in the area of modern Hungary and Austria.</note>. The Picts, Saxons, Scots, and Attacotti harassed the Britons with constant misery. The Austoriani and other Moorish tribes were invading Africa more fiercely than usual. Predatory bands of Goths were plundering Thrace. The King of the Persians was laying hands on the Armenians." <note>original: "Hoc tempore... velut per universum orbem Romanum bellicum canentibus buccinis, excitæ gentes sævissimæ limites sibi proximos persultabant: Gallias Rhætiasque simul Alemanni populabantur: Sarmatæ Pannonias & Quadi: Picti, Saxones, & Scoti & Attacotti Britannos ærumnis vexavêre continuis: Austoriani, Mauricæque aliæ gentes Africam solito acriùs incursabant: Thracias diripiebant prædatorii globi Gotthorum: Persarum Rex manus Armeniis injectabat."</note> 

While the Emperors were busy repelling these enemies, the *Huns*, *Alans*, and *Goths* crossed the *Danube* in two groups, defeated and killed *Valens*, and carried out such a great slaughter of the *Roman* army that *Ammianus* says: "No battle in our Annals, except for that of Cannae <note>A famous battle in 216 BC where Hannibal nearly destroyed the Roman army; here used to emphasize the scale of the defeat at Adrianople in 378 AD.</note>, is recorded to have resulted in such total destruction." <note>original: "Nec ulla Annalibus præter Cannensem ita ad internecionem res legitur gesta."</note> 

These wars were not fully stopped on all sides until the beginning of the reign of *Theodosius*, in 379 and 380 AD. From then on, the Empire remained peaceful and free from foreign armies until his death in 395 AD. During this period, the "four winds" were held back <note>A reference to Revelation 7:1, which describes four angels holding back the four winds of the earth to prevent destruction until the servants of God were sealed.</note>:

### Chap. 3. Apocalypse of St. John.

...was held; and for that duration, there was silence in heaven. The seventh seal was opened when this silence began.

Mr. *Mede* <note>Joseph Mede (1586–1639) was an influential English scholar at Cambridge whose work, *Clavis Apocalyptica*, became a foundational text for Protestant interpretations of biblical prophecy.</note> has explained the *Prophecy* of the first six *trumpets* fairly well. However, if he had observed that the *Prophecy* of pouring out the *vials of wrath* is <term>synchronous</term> <note>original: "synchronal"; occurring at the same time.</note> with the *sounding of the trumpets*, his explanation would have been even more complete.

The name "*Woes*" is given to the wars announced by the final three *trumpets* to distinguish them from the wars of the first four. The sacrifices on the first four days of the Feast of *Tabernacles* <note>A Jewish autumn festival; Newton links the liturgical structure of the Temple service to the timing and sequence of the visions in Revelation.</note>—during which the first four *trumpets* sound and the first four *vials of wrath* are poured out—represent slaughters in four great wars. These wars are represented by four winds from the four corners of the earth. The first was an east wind, the second a west wind, the third a south wind, and the fourth a north wind, in relation to the city of *Rome*, the capital of the old *Roman Empire*. These four plagues fell upon the third part of the *Earth, Sea, Rivers, Sun, Moon, and Stars*; that is, they fell upon the *Earth, Sea, Rivers, Sun, Moon, and Stars* belonging to the third part of the entire geographic scene of these prophecies of *Daniel* and *John*.

He maps these events to the ritual structure of the Jewish Feast of Tabernacles and the four cardinal directions centered on Rome.</summary>

<header>Observations upon the Apocalypse: Part II</header>

<margin>Revelation 8:7, etc.</margin>
The plague of the eastern wind at the sounding of the first trumpet was destined to fall upon the "Earth"—that is, upon the nations of the Greek Empire <note>The Eastern Roman Empire, centered in Constantinople.</note>. Consequently, after the death of Emperor Theodosius the Great <note>Reigned 379–395 AD; his death led to the permanent division of the Roman Empire into East and West.</note>, the Goths, Sarmatians, Huns, Isaurians, and Austorian Moors invaded and terribly ravaged Greece, Thrace, Asia Minor, Armenia, Syria, Egypt, Libya, and Illyricum for ten or twelve years in a row.

The plague of the western wind at the sounding of the second trumpet was destined to fall upon the "Sea," or Western Empire, by means of a *great mountain burning with fire cast into it, and turning it to blood*. Accordingly, in the year 407, that Empire began to be invaded by the Visigoths, Vandals, Alans, Sueves, Burgundians, Ostrogoths, Heruli, Quadi, and Gepids. Through these wars, the empire was broken into ten kingdoms and terribly devastated; Rome itself—the "burning mountain"—was besieged and captured by the Ostrogoths at the beginning of these miseries.

The plague of the southern wind at the sounding of the third trumpet was to cause a *great star, burning as if it were a lamp, to fall from heaven upon the rivers and fountains of waters*. This refers to the Western Empire, now divided into many kingdoms, and was to turn them to wormwood <note>A plant known for its extreme bitterness, symbolizing calamity and sorrow.</note> and blood, making them bitter. Accordingly, Genseric, the King of the Vandals and Alans in Spain,

<page-num>297</page-num>

# Chapter 3. Apocalypse of Saint John

From Spain, in 427 AD <note>original: "A. C." (Anno Christi), used here to mean AD.</note>, he entered *Africa* with an army of eighty thousand men. There, he invaded the *Moors* and made war upon the *Romans*—both in Africa and along the sea coasts of *Europe*—for fifty years together, almost without pause. He captured *Hippo* in 431 AD and *Carthage*, the capital of *Africa*, in 439 AD.

In 455 AD, with a large fleet and an army of three hundred thousand *Vandals* and *Moors*, he invaded *Italy*. He captured and plundered *Rome*, *Naples*, *Capua*, and many other cities, carrying their wealth and the finest of the people <note>original: "flower of the people," referring to the young, healthy, or noble citizens.</note> back into *Africa*. The following year, 456 AD, he tore all of *Africa* away from the Empire, completely expelling the *Romans*. 

Then the *Vandals* invaded and captured the islands of the *Mediterranean*: *Sicily*, *Sardinia*, *Corsica*, *Ebusus* <note>The modern island of Ibiza.</note>, *Majorca*, *Minorca*, and others. In 472 AD, *Ricimer* <note>A powerful Germanic general who effectively controlled the Western Roman Empire.</note> besieged the Emperor *Anthemius* in *Rome*, took the city, and gave the plunder to his soldiers. Around the same time, the *Visigoths* drove the *Romans* out of *Spain*. 

By now, the *Western Emperor*—the *great star which fell from heaven, burning as if it were a lamp*—had gradually lost almost all his dominions through these many wars. Finally, in 476 AD, he was invaded and conquered in a single year by *Odoacer*, King of the *Heruli*. After this, the *Moors* revolted in 477 AD; they weakened the *Vandals* through several wars and took *Mauritania* <note>A region in North Africa corresponding to parts of modern-day Morocco and Algeria.</note> from them.

Through all these wars, *Africa* was almost depopulated, according to *Procopius* <note>A prominent 6th-century Byzantine scholar and historian.</note>, who estimates that more than five million men perished in them. When the *Vandals* first invaded *Africa*, that country was very populous, consisting of about 700 bishoprics <note>The district or diocese under the jurisdiction of a bishop.</note>—more than were in all *France*, *Spain*, and *Italy* combined. However, because of the wars between the *Vandals*, *Romans*, and *Moors*, it was depopulated to such an extent that *Procopius* tells us it was nearly a miracle for a traveler to see a man.

> ...enemies of the holy law, that they must be punished with the penalty of both confiscation of property and death <note>original Latin: "sanguinis" (blood)</note>, if they should attempt to meet again in public with the detestable boldness of their crime. Given on August 25 <note>original Latin: "viii. Kal. Sept." (the eighth day before the Kalends of September)</note>, during the consulship of the illustrious Varane, 410 AD.

This Edict was reinforced five years later by the following:

->Emperors Honorius and Theodosius, Augusti, to Heraclianus, Count of Africa.<-

> Let all those who have crept into their own rites through the superstitions of heresy—those enemies of the most holy law—know that they are to be punished with the penalty of both confiscation and death <note>original Latin: "sanguinis"</note> if they attempt to meet in public again through the boldness of practicing their crime; this is so that true and divine reverence may not be defiled by any contagion. Given on August 25, during the tenth consulship of Honorius and the sixth of Theodosius, Augusti, 415 AD.

These Edicts, being directed to the governor of Africa, applied only to the Africans. Before these, there were many severe decrees against the *Donatists* <note>A Christian sect in North Africa that broke away from the Roman Church over disagreements regarding the purity of the clergy</note>, but they did not involve the death penalty <note>original: "did not extend to blood"</note>. These two were the first that made their meetings, and the meetings of all dissenters, a capital offense. In these Edicts, the term "heretics" refers to all dissenters, as is clear from the following decree against *Euresius*, a *Luciferan* <note>Followers of Lucifer of Cagliari, a group known for their strictness toward those who had formerly compromised their faith under pressure</note> Bishop.

->*Emperors Arcadius and Honorius, Augusti, to Aurelianus, Proconsul of Africa.*<-

Those who are found to deviate from the judgment and path of the Catholic religion, even by a slight argument, are included under the term "heretics" and must submit to the sanctions enacted against them. Therefore, let your office <note>original Latin: "experientia tua"</note> recognize that Euresius is a heretic. Given on September 3 <note>original Latin: "iii. Non. Sept."</note> at Constantinople, during the consulship of Olybrius and Probinus, 395 AD. <note>original Latin: "Hæreticorum vocabulo continentur, & latis adversus eos sanctionibus debent succumbere, qui vel levi argumento à judicio Catholicæ religionis & tramite detecti fuerint deviare : ideoque experientia tua Euresium hæreticum esse cognoscat."</note>

The Greek Emperor *Zeno* adopted *Theoderic*, King of the *Ostrogoths*, as his son, and appointed him <term>master of the horse</term> <note>original: "master of the horse," a high-ranking military office (Magister Equitum)</note> and *Patrician*, and Consul of *Constantinople*. Entrusting the *Roman* people and Senate to him, Zeno gave him the *Western Empire* and sent him into *Italy* against *Odoacer*, King of the *Heruli*. *Theoderic* then led his nation into *Italy*, conquered *Odoacer*, and reigned over *Italy*, *Sicily*, *Rhætia* <note>a province in the central Alps</note>, *Noricum* <note>a province in modern-day Austria and Slovenia</note>, *Dalmatia*, *Liburnia*, *Istria*, and parts of *Suevia*, *Pannonia*, and *Gaul* <note>original: "Gallia"</note>. Because of this, *Ennodius* <note>Magnus Felix Ennodius (c. 473–521 AD), a bishop and poet who wrote a famous panegyric, or speech of praise, to Theoderic</note> said in a speech to *Theoderic*: "The Roman kingdoms have returned to their former borders" <note>original Latin: *Ad limitem suum Romana regna remeâsse*</note>. *Theoderic* reigned with great wisdom, moderation, and success. He treated the *Romans* with exceptional kindness, governed them by their own laws, and restored their government under their own Senate and Consuls, with he himself supplying the

<header>Chap. 3. Apocalypse of St. John.</header>

[He held the] place of Emperor, without assuming the title. *Procopius* <note>a prominent Byzantine scholar and historian</note> says, "He ruled his subjects in such a manner that no honor truly fitting for an Emperor was lacking to him: his devotion to justice was great, and his custody of the laws was diligent: he kept his lands untouched by neighboring barbarians, etc." <note>original Latin: "Ita sibi parentibus præfuit, ut vere Imperatori conveniens decus nullum ipsi abesset : Justitiæ magnus ei cultus, legumque diligens custodia : terras à vicinis barbaris servavit intactas, &c."</note> For this reason, I do not count the reign of this King among the plagues of the four winds <note>a reference to the apocalyptic judgments described in the Book of Revelation</note>.

The plague of the northern wind, signaled by the sounding of the fourth trumpet, was meant to darken the *Sun*, *Moon*, and *Stars*—that is, the King, the kingdom, and the Princes of the *Western Empire*—and to keep them in darkness for some time. Accordingly, after *Belisarius* <note>the leading general of the Byzantine Empire under Justinian I</note> conquered the *Vandals*, he invaded *Italy* in 535 AD. He waged war against the *Ostrogoths* in *Dalmatia*, *Liburnia*, *Venetia*, *Lombardy*, *Tuscany*, and other regions north of *Rome* for twenty consecutive years.

In this war, many cities were captured and recaptured. In retaking *Milan* <note>original: "Millain"</note> from the *Romans*, the *Ostrogoths* killed all the males, both young and old—amounting to three hundred thousand, according to *Procopius's* estimate—and sent the women as captives to their allies, the *Burgundians*. *Rome* itself was taken and retaken several times, which thinned the population; the old government by a Senate ceased, the nobility were ruined, and all the glory of the city was extinguished. In 552 AD, after a war of seven-

->The Emperors Arcadius and Honorius, Augusti, to Aurelianus, Proconsul of Africa.<-

> Those who are found to deviate, even by a slight argument, from the judgment and path of the Catholic religion are included under the name of "heretics" and must submit to the legal penalties issued against them. Therefore, let your experience recognize Euresius to be a heretic. Issued on September 3rd <note>original: "iii. Non. Sept."</note> at Constantinople, during the consulship of Olybrius and Probinus [395 AD]. <note>original Latin: "Hæreticorum vocabulo continentur, & latis adversus eos sanctionibus debent succumbere, qui vel levi argumento à judicio Catholicæ religionis & tramite detecti fuerint deviare : ideoque experientia tua Euresium hæreticum esse cognoscat. Dat. iii. Non. Sept. Constantinop. Olybrio & Probino Coss. A. C. 395."</note>

The Greek Emperor Zeno adopted Theoderic, King of the Ostrogoths, as his son, made him master of the horse and a *Patrician* <note>a high-ranking honorary title in the later Roman Empire</note>, and Consul of Constantinople. Recommending the Roman people and Senate to him, Zeno gave him the Western Empire and sent him into Italy against Odoacer, King of the Heruli. Theoderic then led his nation into Italy, conquered Odoacer, and reigned over Italy, Sicily, Raetia <note>a Roman province covering parts of modern Switzerland, Germany, and Austria</note>, Noricum <note>an ancient kingdom and later Roman province in modern Austria and Slovenia</note>, Dalmatia, Liburnia, Istria, and parts of Swabia <note>original: "Suevia"</note>, Pannonia <note>an ancient province in Central Europe</note>, and Gaul <note>original: "Gallia"</note>. For this reason, Ennodius <note>a bishop and poet of the 5th-6th century</note> said in a speech of praise to Theoderic: "The Roman kingdoms have returned to their own borders." <note>original Latin: "Ad limitem suum Romana regna remeâsse."</note>

Theoderic reigned with great wisdom, moderation, and prosperity. He treated the Romans with exceptional kindness, governed them by their own laws, and restored their government under their own Senate and Consuls, while he himself filled the
place

"He ruled over his subjects like a father," says *Procopius* <note>a prominent 6th-century Byzantine scholar and historian</note>, "so that no honor truly befitting an Emperor was lacking in him. He had a great devotion to justice and a diligent keeping of the laws; he kept his lands untouched by neighboring barbarians, etc." <note>original Latin: "Ita sibi parentibus præfuit, ut vere Imperatori conveniens decus nullum ipsi abesset: Justitiæ magnus ei cultus, legumque diligens custodia: terras à vicinis barbaris servavit intactas, &c."</note> For this reason, I do not count the reign of this King among the plagues of the four winds <note>a reference to the destructive forces mentioned in the Book of Revelation</note>.

The plague of the northern wind, at the sounding of the fourth trumpet, was meant to cause the *Sun*, *Moon*, and *Stars*—that is, the King, kingdom, and Princes of the *Western Empire*—to be darkened, and to remain in darkness for some time. Accordingly, *Belisarius*, having conquered the *Vandals*, invaded *Italy* in 535 AD and waged war against the *Ostrogoths* in *Dalmatia*, *Liburnia*, *Venetia*, *Lombardy*, *Tuscany*, and other regions north of *Rome* for twenty years together. In this war, many cities were taken and retaken. While retaking *Milan* <note>original: "Millain"</note> from the *Romans*, the *Ostrogoths* killed all the males, young and old—amounting to three hundred thousand, as *Procopius* calculates—and sent the women as captives to their allies, the *Burgundians*. *Rome* itself was taken and retaken several times, and thereby the population was thinned; the old government by a Senate ceased, the nobles were ruined, and all the glory of the city was extinguished. In 552 AD, after a war of seventeen...

This was followed by the war of the *Lombards*, the fiercest of all the *Barbarians*, which began in 568 AD and lasted for thirty-eight years; "such a slaughter occurred," says *Anastasius*, "as no one remembers from ages past" <note>original Latin: "factâ tali clade, qualem à sæculo nullus meminit"</note>. It ended at last during the Papacy of *Sabinian* in 605 AD, with a peace then made with the *Lombards*. Three years before this war ended, *Gregory the Great*, then Bishop of *Rome*, spoke of it in this way:

> For how we are oppressed by daily swords and by such great incursions of the Lombards—behold, for thirty-five years now—we cannot express with any words of petition <note>original Latin: "Qualiter enim & quotidianis gladiis & quantis Longobardorum incursionibus, ecce jam per triginta quinque annorum longitudinem premimur, nullis explere vocibus suggestionis valemus"</note>:

And in one of his sermons to the people, he expressed the great loss of the *Romans* in these wars in this manner:

> You see how few of you remain from that countless multitude, and yet even now daily punishments drive us, sudden accidents oppress us, and new events and unforeseen disasters afflict us <note>original Latin: "Ex illa plebe innumerabili quanti remanseritis aspicitis, & tamen adhuc quotidiè flagella urgent, repentini casus opprimunt, novæ res & improvisæ clades affligunt"</note>.

In another sermon, he described the desolations in this way:

> Cities are destroyed, fortresses are overthrown, fields are depopulated, and the land is reduced to a wasteland. There are no inhabitants in the fields, and almost none [left in the cities...] <note>original Latin: "Destructæ urbes, eversa sunt castra, depopulati agri, in solitudinem terra redacta est. Nullus in agris incola, penè nullus"</note>

All of this was spoken by *Gregory* to the people of *Rome*, who were witnesses to the truth of it. [Eight]een years later, the kingdom of the **Ostrogoths** <note>A Germanic people who established a kingdom in Italy in the late 5th century</note> fell; yet the remnants of the Ostrogoths, along with an army of **Germans** called in to assist them, continued the war for three or four years longer. This was followed by the war of the **Lombards**, the fiercest of all the Barbarians, which began in AD 568 and lasted for thirty-eight years. "Such a slaughter was made," says Anastasius, "as no one remembered from ages past" <note>original Latin: *factâ tali clade, qualem à sæculo nullus meminit*</note>. This period ended at last during the papacy of **Sabinian** in AD 605, with a peace treaty then made with the Lombards. 

Three years before this war ended, **Gregory the Great**, then Bishop of Rome, spoke of it in this way: "For we are weighed down by daily swords and by such great incursions of the Lombards—now for the length of thirty-five years—that we are unable to express our plight with any words of petition" <note>original Latin: *Qualiter enim & quotidianis gladiis & quantis Longobardorum incursionibus, ecce jam per triginta quinque annorum longitudinem premimur, nullis explere vocibus suggestionis valemus*</note>. 

In one of his sermons to the people, he expressed the great loss of Roman lives in these wars: "You see how many of you remain from that once-countless population; and yet, the lashes of punishment still strike daily, sudden misfortunes oppress us, and new events and unforeseen disasters afflict us" <note>original Latin: *Ex illa plebe innumerabili quanti remanseritis aspicitis, & tamen adhuc quotidiè flagella urgent, repentini casus opprimunt, novæ res & improvisæ clades affligunt*</note>. In another sermon, he described the desolation: "Cities are destroyed, fortresses are overturned, fields are depopulated, and the land is reduced to a wilderness. No inhabitant remains in the fields, almost no one..." <note>original Latin: *Destructæ urbes, eversa sunt castra, depopulati agri, in solitudinem terra redacta est. Nullus in agris incola, penè nullus*</note>

This pit was opened to let out smoke and locusts into the regions of the four monarchies <note>The four world empires of Daniel's prophecy: Babylon, Medo-Persia, Greece, and Rome.</note>, or some of them. Swarms of locusts often arise in *Arabia Felix* <note>original: "Arabia fælix" — Latin for "Happy Arabia," the fertile southern part of the Arabian Peninsula.</note>, and from there they infest the neighboring nations. Thus, they are a very appropriate symbol of the numerous armies of *Arabians* invading the *Romans*. 

They began to invade them in 634 AD <note>original: "A. C. 634" — Anno Christi</note>, and to reign at *Damascus* in 637 AD. # Observations upon the [Revelation of St. John]

The seven days are alluded to by the Lamb opening the seven seals one after another.

On the tenth day of the month <note>Tishrei, the seventh month of the Hebrew calendar.</note>, a young bullock was offered as a sin offering for the High Priest, and a goat as a sin offering for the people. Lots were cast upon two goats to determine which of them would be God’s lot for the sin offering; the other goat was called <term>Azazel</term>, the <term>scapegoat</term> <note>original: "ſcape-goat"; a goat symbolically burdened with the sins of the people and sent into the wild.</note>. The High Priest, dressed in his linen garments, took a <term>censer</term> <note>original: "cenſer"; a vessel for burning incense.</note> full of burning coals from the Altar, his hand being full of sweet incense ground into small pieces. He went into the Most Holy Place within the veil and put the incense upon the fire. He then sprinkled the blood of the bullock with his finger upon the <term>mercy seat</term> <note>original: "mercy-ſeat"; the gold lid of the Ark of the Covenant, representing the throne of God's presence.</note> and in front of the mercy seat seven times.

Then he killed the goat that fell to God’s lot as a sin offering for the people. He brought its blood within the veil and sprinkled it also seven times upon the mercy seat and in front of the mercy seat. Then he went out to the Altar and sprinkled it also seven times with the blood of the bullock, and just as many times with the blood of the goat. *After this, he laid both his hands upon the head of the live goat and confessed over him all the iniquities of the children of Israel, and all their transgressions in all their sins, putting them upon the head of the goat; and sent him away into the wilderness by the*

# Apocalypse of St. John.

In the interpretation of this Prophecy, the *woman in heaven clothed with the sun*, before she flies into the wilderness, represents the primitive universal <note>original: "catholick"</note> Church. She is illuminated by the *seven lamps in the seven golden lampstands* <note>original: "candlesticks"</note>, which are the *seven Churches of Asia*. The Dragon represents the same empire as *Daniel’s* "he-goat" <note>A reference to the vision in Daniel 8 regarding the succession of empires</note> during the reign of its last horn—that is, the entire Roman Empire until it was divided into the *Greek* and *Latin* Empires. Throughout the time of that division, the Dragon signifies the *Greek* Empire alone, while the Beast is *Daniel's* fourth beast <note>A reference to the vision of four beasts in Daniel 7, symbolizing four great worldly empires</note>—namely, the Empire of the *Latins*. <margin>A vertical line is marked in red pencil next to this section.</margin> Before the division of the *Roman* Empire into the *Greek* and *Latin* Empires, the Beast is included within the body of the Dragon; from the time of that division onward, the Beast represents the *Latin* Empire only. 

Because of this, the Dragon and the Beast share the same heads and horns; however, the heads are crowned on the Dragon, and the horns are crowned on the Beast. These horns represent ten kingdoms into which the Beast was divided shortly after its separation from the Dragon, as has been described previously. The heads represent seven successive dynasties, or parts, into which the *Roman* Empire became divided upon the opening of the seven seals. Before the woman fled into

<meta>This page continues Newton's interpretation of the Beast from the Book of Revelation, specifically analyzing the "seven kings" mentioned in Revelation 17. Kings: *five have fallen, and one is, and the other has not yet come; and when he comes, he must continue for a short time. And the Beast that was and is not, even he is the eighth, and is of the seven, and goes into destruction <note>original: perdition</note>.* Five have fallen, the times of the first five seals <note>In Revelation, the opening of seven seals on a scroll represents successive historical periods or divine judgments.</note> being past; and "one is," meaning the time of the sixth seal is being considered as the present; and another has not yet come, and when he comes—which will be at the opening of the seventh seal—he must continue for a short time. And the Beast that was and is not, even he is the eighth, by means of the division of the Roman Empire into two collateral <note>Side-by-side or parallel, referring to the Western and Eastern Roman Empires.</note> Empires; and he is "of the seven," being one half of the seventh, and shall go into destruction <note>original: perdition</note>.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;The words, *five have fallen, and one is, and the other has not yet come*, are usually referred to by interpreters as the time of *John* the Apostle, when the prophecy was given. However, it must be considered that in this prophecy many things are spoken of as if they were present, which were not actually present when the prophecy was given, but which would be present with respect to some future time that is treated as the "present" within the visions. Thus, where it is said upon the pouring out of the seventh vial of wrath <note>The "vials" or "bowls" of wrath are the final set of judgments in the Book of Revelation.</note> that *great Babylon was remembered before God, to give to her the cup of the wine of the fierceness of His wrath*, this relates not to the time of *John* the Apostle,

<meta>catchword: Apostle,</meta>

<summary>Newton interprets the "seven kings" of Revelation as periods defined by the opening of the Seven Seals, explaining that the prophecy's "present tense" refers to the chronological perspective of the vision rather than the Apostle John's lifetime.</summary>
<keywords>Seven Seals, Seven Kings, Roman Empire, Division of Empire, Seventh Vial, Babylon, Prophetic Time, Isaac Newton</keywords>

--- Page 351 ---
<meta>This page continues Newton's argument that the Book of Revelation uses a "prophetic present." He explains that when the text says something "is" happening, it refers to the timeframe of the vision itself, not the literal time when the Apostle John was writing in the first century.</meta>

<page-num>322</page-num>

<header>->Observations upon the<-</header>

Apostle, but to the time of pouring out the seventh vial of wrath <note>In the Book of Revelation, the "vials of wrath" represent the final sequence of divine judgments poured out upon the earth.</note>. So where it is said, *Babylon is fallen, is fallen*; and *thrust in your sickle and reap, for the time has come for you to reap*; and *the time of the dead has come, that they should be judged*; and again, *I saw the dead, small and great, stand before God*: these sayings relate not to the days of *John* the Apostle, but to the later times considered as "present" within the visions. 

In the same way, the words, *five have fallen, and one is, and the other has not yet come, and the Beast that was and is not, he is the eighth*, are not to be referred to the age of *John* the Apostle. Instead, they relate to the time when the Beast was to be wounded to death with a sword, and show that this wound was to be given to him in his sixth head; without this reference, we are not told in which head the Beast was wounded. 

<margin>☌</margin> *And the ten horns which you saw are ten kings, who have received no kingdom as yet, but receive power as kings for one hour with the Beast. <meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Newton explains that prophecies in Revelation use a shifting "present tense" to describe historical events—such as the wounding of the Beast's sixth head—as they occur within the vision's timeline. He identifies the "ten horns" as ten kings who support the Beast and eventually war against the Lamb.</summary>

<keywords>Isaac Newton, Book of Revelation, Prophecy, The Beast, Ten Horns, Seven Vials, Whore of Babylon, Eschatology, Church History</keywords>

--- Page 352 ---
<meta>This page concludes Isaac Newton’s interpretation of the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse), specifically focusing on the imagery of the Whore of Babylon and the Beast. Newton argues that the "prophetic present" refers to the time of the visions' fulfillment rather than the Apostle John's own time.</meta>

<page-num>323</page-num>

<header>Apocalypse of St. John.</header>

*...and chosen and faithful. And he says to me, the waters which you saw where the whore sits are peoples, multitudes, nations, and tongues, who make up her Beast. And the ten horns which you saw on the Beast, these will hate the whore and will make her desolate and naked; they will eat her flesh and burn her with fire at the end of the 1,260 days <note>1,260 days: A period frequently mentioned in the books of Daniel and Revelation. Newton and his contemporaries often interpreted these "prophetic days" as years.</note>. For God has put it in their hearts to fulfill his will, and to agree to give their kingdom to the Beast until the words of God are fulfilled. And the woman whom you saw is that great city which reigns over the kings of the earth—or the great city of the Latins <note>the Latins: Newton identifies the "great city" as Rome, the seat of the Latin-speaking Church, which he believed exercised authority over the fragmented kingdoms of Europe.</note>, which reigns over the ten kings until the end of those days.*

---

->T H E  E N D.<- <note>original: "FINIS."</note>

<vocab>Apocalypse, St. John, Beast, 1,260 days, Latins, Kings of the earth, great city</vocab>

<summary>This final page of Newton's commentary identifies the biblical "Whore of Babylon" and "Great City" as Rome and the Latin Church. Newton predicts that the political powers (the ten horns) will eventually turn against this religious authority at the conclusion of the 1,260-year prophetic period.</summary>

<keywords>Isaac Newton, Apocalypse, Book of Revelation, Whore of Babylon, 1260 days, Rome, Papacy, Prophecy, Ten Kings</keywords>

--- Page 353 ---
<meta>This page concludes Isaac Newton’s interpretation of the Book of Revelation (the Apocalypse), continuing the analysis of the Beast and the ten kings. The upper portion of this page is illegible due to ink bleeding through from the reverse side, leaving only the final passage of the text readable.</meta>

<page-num>334</page-num>

<warning>The text on this page is extremely faint; the upper half is dominated by mirrored bleed-through from the reverse side.</warning>

<note>The following right-reading text appears below a large section of mirrored ghosting from page 333. The text begins mid-sentence.</note>

...the wound was to be given him in his last days. And without this reference, we can find nothing regarding the head that the Beast lost. <note>original: "nothing in the head the Beast lost." This refers to the "deadly wound" described in Revelation 13:3, which Newton interprets as a specific historical or political setback for the Roman power.</note>

And the ten horns which you saw are ten kings, who have received no kingdom as of yet, but receive power as kings for one hour with the Beast. <note>one hour: In prophetic interpretation, this often signifies a very short or specific period of time rather than a literal sixty minutes.</note> These are of one mind—being all of the same religion—and shall give their power and strength to the Beast. These shall make war with the Lamb <note>the Lamb: A common biblical title for Jesus Christ.</note> at the sounding of the last trumpet, and the Lamb shall overcome them; for he is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those who are with him are called, and <note>The text ends abruptly here, though the biblical verse (Revelation 17:14) concludes with "chosen and faithful."</note>

---

->T H E  E N D<-
<note>original: "F I N I S"</note>

<vocab>Beast, ten horns, Kings, Lamb, Lord of Lords, King of Kings, last trumpet</vocab>

<summary>This final fragment of Newton's commentary on Revelation describes the alliance between ten kings and the "Beast," their shared religious purpose, and their ultimate defeat by the "Lamb" (Christ) at the end of time.</summary>
<keywords>Isaac Newton, Book of Revelation, prophecy, the Beast, Ten Kings, the Lamb, End Times, Apocalypse, biblical interpretation</keywords>
